\"\"\"Assert the subprocess exit code (COMP-010.4).\n\n        Default behaviour asserts ``equals=0``. ``equals`` and ``in_set``\n        are mutually exclusive — supplying both raises ``ValueError`` {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Jnw2hnDzpPQmuxmLQoy3WD","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D-0069 — Implementation notes\n2\t\n3\t## Why this task was test-only\n4\t\n5\tT04.01 (D-0064) shipped the full `Expect.stderr` / `Expect.stdout`\n6\tbodies alongside the package skeleton. Both primitives already\n7\tsupported:\n8\t\n9\t* Three named arguments: `contains`, `regex`, `not_contains` (each\n10\t  `Optional[str]`, default `None`).\n11\t* A shared `_stream_predicate` engine so any regression in one surfaces\n12\t  on both.\n13\t* Defensive ANSI re-strip via `_strip_ansi` → `ANSI_ESCAPE_RE` so raw\n14\t  transcript fixtures match clean.\n15\t* Failure payloads with `expected` / `actual` (DM-005) and a\n16\t  primitive-tagged callable name for JSONL logging.\n17\t\n18\tT04.07's deliverable is the **per-primitive acceptance harness** that\n19\ttouches every named argument and the ANSI-strip invariant on both\n20\tstreams. No body changes were required to\n21\t`src/superclaude/cli/eval/expect.py`.\n22\t\n23\t## Decisions\n24\t\n25\t1. **Double-strip ANSI on the predicate side, even though PtyStream\n26\t   already strips upstream.** PtyStream is the on-the-wire producer,\n27\t   but `ctx.stdout` / `ctx.stderr` are typed `str` and could in theory\n28\t   be sourced from a non-PtyStream path in the future (manifest-loaded\n29\t   transcript fixtures, test-injected buffers, replay tooling). The\n30\t   defensive re-strip costs one regex `sub` per assertion and removes\n31\t   an entire failure mode where a hidden `\\x1B[0m` would silently\n32\t   break a substring check. `test_stdout_strips_ansi_csi_before_match`\n33\t   pins both the match side (PASS) and the leak side (FAIL on a\n34\t   literal escape) so a future producer that *fails* to strip can't\n35\t   regress the contract.\n36\t\n37\t2. **Evaluation order: `contains` → `regex` → `not_contains`.** When\n38\t   multiple predicates are supplied, the first failing check produces\n39\t   the failure payload — the rest short-circuit. This matches the\n40\t   declaration order in the signature so manifest authors reading\n41\t   `Expect.stdout(contains=..., regex=..., not_contains=...)` see the\n42\t   same priority the runtime applies.\n43\t   `test_stdout_contains_runs_before_regex` pins it.\n44\t\n45\t3. **No-args is a degenerate PASS.** `Expect.stdout()` with no\n46\t   predicates returns `passed=True` rather than raising a\n47\t   \"no-predicate\" error at construction time. Rationale: manifest\n48\t   authors sometimes want a sentinel assertion that the stream is\n49\t   *reachable* (the EvalContext was populated) without making any\n50\t   content claim. A construction-time raise would force them to invent\n51\t   a no-op regex (`r\".\"` or similar) just to satisfy the API.\n52\t   `test_stdout_no_args_passes_on_empty_buffer` and\n53\t   `test_stderr_no_args_passes_on_empty_buffer` pin the no-op contract.\n54\t\n55\t4. **`actual` field carries the *full* stripped buffer, not a truncation.**\n56\t   The Reporter (T03.13) renders unified diffs over the buffer when\n57\t   building the failure section; truncation at the predicate layer\n58\t   would force the Reporter to either re-fetch the artifact or emit\n59\t   incomplete context. Memory is bounded by the per-eval log cap\n60\t   enforced upstream by the orchestrator (T03.15), so handing the full\n61\t   buffer down is safe.\n62\t\n63\t5. **Symmetric test matrix across the two primitives.** Both streams\n64\t   get all 11 argument-coverage cases (contains × pass/fail, regex ×\n65\t   pass/fail, not_contains × pass/fail, plus the ANSI-strip variants\n66\t   on both, plus the envelope + declarative checks). Originally I\n67\t   considered parametrizing across stream names to halve the file, but\n68\t   the asymmetric ANSI variants (CSI on stdout, OSC on stderr) and the\n69\t   distinct fixture inputs read more clearly as named tests. The\n   defensive re-strip costs one regex `sub` per assertion and removes\n   an entire failure mode where a hidden `\\x1B[0m` would silently\n   break a substring check. `test_stdout_strips_ansi_csi_before_match`\n   pins both the match side (PASS) and the leak side (FAIL on a\n   literal escape) so a future producer that *fails* to strip can't\n   regress the contract.\n\n2. **Evaluation order: `contains` → `regex` → `not_contains`.** When\n   multiple predicates are supplied, the first failing check produces\n   the failure payload — the rest short-circuit. This matches the\n   declaration order in the signature so manifest authors reading\n   `Expect.stdout(contains=..., regex=..., not_contains=...)` see the\n   same priority the runtime applies.\n   `test_stdout_contains_runs_before_regex` pins it.\n\n3. **No-args is a degenerate PASS.** `Expect.stdout()` with no\n   predicates returns `passed=True` rather than raising a\n   \"no-predicate\" error at construction time. Rationale: manifest\n   authors sometimes want a sentinel assertion that the stream is\n   *reachable* (the EvalContext was populated) without making any\n   content claim. A construction-time raise would force them to invent\n   a no-op regex (`r\".\"` or similar) just to satisfy the API.\n   `test_stdout_no_args_passes_on_empty_buffer` and\n   `test_stderr_no_args_passes_on_empty_buffer` pin the no-op contract.\n\n4. **`actual` field carries the *full* stripped buffer, not a truncation.**\n   The Reporter (T03.13) renders unified diffs over the buffer when\n   building the failure section; truncation at the predicate layer\n   would force the Reporter to either re-fetch the artifact or emit\n   incomplete context. Memory is bounded by the per-eval log cap\n   enforced upstream by the orchestrator (T03.15), so handing the full\n   buffer down is safe.\n\n5. **Symmetric test matrix across the two primitives.** Both streams\n   get all 11 argument-coverage cases (contains × pass/fail, regex ×\n   pass/fail, not_contains × pass/fail, plus the ANSI-strip variants\n   on both, plus the envelope + declarative checks). Originally I\n   considered parametrizing across stream names to halve the file, but\n   the asymmetric ANSI variants (CSI on stdout, OSC on stderr) and the\n   distinct fixture inputs read more clearly as named tests. A future\n   change that adds a 4th argument can drop into the same matrix\n   without disturbing the existing layout.\n\n## What was NOT changed in src/\n\n* `Expect.stderr` and `Expect.stdout` bodies in\n  `src/superclaude/cli/eval/expect.py` were already complete after\n  T04.01.
